A grown-up-sized long-sleeved, ankle-length, drop-hood zip-front onesie, with a big attached hood, with open cuffs and bare hands, and with sewn-in bootie feet, in bamboo-blend jersey, a real plain stretch suit and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a lightly distressed worn-in print, designed to make the onesie look like the outfit of an Inuit hunter. The outfit's design is wrapped around the entire suit as one continuous print from the collar to the ankles, wrapping around the sides to the back. The print shows a hooded fur parka, fur trousers, with a fur hood printed on the hood, fur boots printed on the feet, and the sleeves end in plain cuffs with no hands printed. Front and back are both fully printed, the back showing how the outfit looks from behind. No background colour or plain jersey shows around the print, on the shoulders, sleeves or legs. The print is applied to the suit's own pattern pieces, front, back, each sleeve and each leg are fully covered in the matching cloth of the outfit, with no leftover background colour between them. There are no printed arms, hands, fingers, legs or feet and no printed skin on the suit, except a small printed neckline detail where the outfit is open. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The print is cut exactly to the suit, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The outfit is laid out on the suit's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. It is not a drawing of a person standing in the middle of the suit, no body, no head, no limbs and no figure are pictured, only the outfit's cloth, trims and fastenings spread over the whole suit. The printed top of fur trousers reaching the hem and ending there. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the collar and lapels in faux leather, the belt and buckle in chenille and the epaulettes in faux fur, standing out from the surface. Printed and plush together, the outfit is printed all over, with padded appliqué parts standing out from the surface. It is a complete costume of Inuit hunter in one piece, shown full length from collar to ankles, with a hooded fur parka, fur trousers on the legs, a fur hood (as the hood), fur boots (as the feet), a harpoon sewn or attached to the suit. The upper half carries the top and the leg tubes carry the lower garment, so the whole outfit reads at once. Novelty embellishments, a bold embroidered emblem across the chest, rhinestone buttons down the front, ruffled fabric frills along the seams and braided cord drawstrings with knotted ends. It reads as a joyful, exaggerated novelty onesie, stuffed with texture and detail.

The whole suit is the outfit, front, back, arms and legs (and any hood, gloves or feet) are printed as the matching parts of it, with the seams and the zip hidden in the pattern. The print shows a pinstripe suit, trousers, with a fedora printed on the hood, with no footwear shown because the suit is footless, and printed gloves on the attached gloves at the sleeve ends. The back of the suit shows the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge, from the collar or hood to the ankles or feet. The outfit's own cloth reaches every seam, cuff and hem with no border or backdrop. The outfit's cloth wraps the whole suit like a pattern, with the body, the outside and inside of each sleeve and each leg, and the sides, so there are no plain coloured side panels or background patches anywhere. There are no printed arms, hands, fingers, legs or feet and no printed skin on the suit, except a small printed neckline detail where the outfit is open. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The print is cut exactly to the suit, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The outfit is laid out on the suit's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. It is not a drawing of a person standing in the middle of the suit, no body, no head, no limbs and no figure are pictured, only the outfit's cloth, trims and fastenings spread over the whole suit. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the belt and buckle in velvet, the pockets in faux leather and the collar and lapels in quilted padding, standing out from the surface. The print gives the whole outfit, and raised layers of soft fabric are sewn on over it for the collar, pockets, belt, buttons and badges, so the suit is half picture and half sculpture. The suit is the whole 1920s gangster outfit, not just a top, with a pinstripe suit, trousers on the legs, a fedora (as the hood), spats (as the feet), a violin case sewn or attached to the suit. It is shown in full, from the neckline down to the ankle cuffs. Novelty embellishments, embroidered badges and crests with stitched lettering, ruffled fabric frills along the seams, satin ribbon bows and piping and beaded fringe along the hem. The embellishments are sewn on as real raised fabric, so the suit has depth, texture and sparkle from every angle.

The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the suit is outfit cloth. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The outfit is laid out on the suit's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. No standing figure, torso, arms or legs are depicted; the suit's own shape is the body, and the print is only the outfit's fabric covering it from edge to edge. The outfit's design is wrapped around the entire suit as one continuous print from the collar to the ankles, wrapping around the sides to the back. The print shows a fur-lined robe over a tunic, with a crown printed on the hood, boots printed on the feet, and printed gloves on the attached gloves at the sleeve ends. Front and back are both fully printed, the back showing how the outfit looks from behind. There is no separate background, the print is cut to the onesie's shape. The outfit's cloth wraps the whole suit like a pattern, with the body, the outside and inside of each sleeve and each leg, and the sides, so there are no plain coloured side panels or background patches anywhere. No limbs are printed on the suit, bare arms and legs are never drawn as skin on the fabric, and the suit is simply cut short or sleeveless wherever the outfit would show them. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The outfit's collar meets the real collar band, its shoulders sit on the real shoulder seams and its sleeves fill the real sleeves, all in one piece of printed cloth. The silhouette is a plain one-piece suit, with every part of the outfit printed inside its shape and none beyond it. The outfit is stretched to the suit's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. No standing figure, torso, arms or legs are depicted; the suit's own shape is the body, and the print is only the outfit's fabric covering it from edge to edge. It may also show flat printed chest hair on __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ showing at the open collar, all as flat ink. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the buttons in velvet, the badges and patches in quilted padding and the medals and ribbons in fleece, standing out from the surface. Printed and plush together, the outfit is printed all over, with padded appliqué parts standing out from the surface. A full medieval king costume, head to toe in a single suit, with a fur-lined robe over a tunic, a crown (as the hood), boots (as the feet), a sceptre sewn or attached to the suit. Nothing of the outfit is missing and the legs of the suit show the lower garment. Novelty embellishments, metallic foil-print accents that catch the light, dangling fabric tassels on the zip pull and hood, a quilted belt-like waist band with a big buckle shape and chunky pom-pom trim along the hood edge and cuffs. The embellishments are sewn on as real raised fabric, so the suit has depth, texture and sparkle from every angle.

Where the outfit leaves arms or legs bare, the suit itself is cut sleeveless, short-sleeved or short-legged at that point, so the wearer's own limbs show; no printed arm, leg, hand, foot or skin appears on the suit's fabric. The outfit's collar meets the real collar band, its shoulders sit on the real shoulder seams and its sleeves fill the real sleeves, all in one piece of printed cloth. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The outfit is laid out on the suit's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. An adult-sized long-sleeved, ankle-length, zip-front onesie, with no hood, just a neckline and collar, with built-in gloves at the sleeve ends, and with built-in slipper feet, in waffle-knit cotton, a real plain stretch suit and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a glossy all-over digital print, designed to make the onesie look like the outfit of a 1970s disco dancer. The outfit's design is wrapped around the entire suit as one continuous print from the collar to the ankles, wrapping around the sides to the back. The print shows a satin shirt open at the collar, flared trousers, platform shoes printed on the feet, and printed gloves on the attached gloves at the sleeve ends. The back of the suit shows the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge, from the collar or hood to the ankles or feet. The outfit's own cloth reaches every seam, cuff and hem with no border or backdrop. Every sleeve and every leg of the suit is a tube covered completely, all the way round, in the outfit's cloth for that limb (sleeve cloth, trouser, tights or leg fabric), so no grey, blue or coloured background shows on the arms, legs, sides or back. There are no printed arms, hands, fingers, legs or feet and no printed skin on the suit, except a small printed neckline detail where the outfit is open. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the suit is outfit cloth. The silhouette is a plain one-piece suit, with every part of the outfit printed inside its shape and none beyond it. The outfit is stretched to the suit's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. No standing figure, torso, arms or legs are depicted; the suit's own shape is the body, and the print is only the outfit's fabric covering it from edge to edge. It may also show a flat printed line of cleavage in __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ at a low or sweetheart neckline, all as flat ink. A printed waistband showing only the top of flared trousers. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the collar and lapels in faux fur, the medals and ribbons in fleece and the badges and patches in chenille, standing out from the surface. Printed and plush together, the outfit is printed all over, with padded appliqué parts standing out from the surface. The suit is the whole 1970s disco dancer outfit, not just a top, with a satin shirt open at the collar, flared trousers on the legs, platform shoes (as the feet). It is shown in full, from the neckline down to the ankle cuffs. Novelty embellishments, chunky pom-pom trim along the hood edge and cuffs, metallic foil-print accents that catch the light, terry-loop textured stripes and oversized novelty buttons in contrasting colours. It is a novelty costume garment, made to be bright, playful, over-the-top and a pleasure to touch.

The hood carries only hood cloth, no face, hair, ears or head is printed on it, and no crowd, audience, stage, lights or scene appears anywhere on the suit, only the outfit's own cloth. The print shows a long white thobe with a cloak, with a turban printed on the hood, sandals printed on the feet, and the sleeves end in plain cuffs with no hands printed. The sleeve hems are cut at an angle, slanted, pointed or curved, to follow the outfit's own cuffs, and the print follows that edge exactly. Front and back are both fully printed, the back showing how the outfit looks from behind. There is no separate background, the print is cut to the onesie's shape. The outfit's cloth wraps the whole suit like a pattern, with the body, the outside and inside of each sleeve and each leg, and the sides, so there are no plain coloured side panels or background patches anywhere. Where the outfit leaves arms or legs bare, the suit itself is cut sleeveless, short-sleeved or short-legged at that point, so the wearer's own limbs show; no printed arm, leg, hand, foot or skin appears on the suit's fabric. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the suit is outfit cloth. The silhouette is a plain one-piece suit, with every part of the outfit printed inside its shape and none beyond it. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided suit shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. The hood carries only hood cloth, no face, hair, ears or head is printed on it, and no crowd, audience, stage, lights or scene appears anywhere on the suit, only the outfit's own cloth. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the medals and ribbons in chenille, the hood trim in felt and the pockets in velvet, standing out from the surface. The print gives the whole outfit, and raised layers of soft fabric are sewn on over it for the collar, pockets, belt, buttons and badges, so the suit is half picture and half sculpture. It is a complete costume of imam in one piece, shown full length from collar to ankles, with a long white thobe with a cloak, a turban (as the hood), sandals (as the feet), prayer beads sewn or attached to the suit. The upper half carries the top and the leg tubes carry the lower garment, so the whole outfit reads at once. Novelty embellishments, lurex-thread embroidery, chunky pom-pom trim along the hood edge and cuffs, hand-stitched patchwork appliqué in bright colours and ruffled fabric frills along the seams. It is a novelty costume garment, made to be bright, playful, over-the-top and a pleasure to touch.
